# \<System Name\> Control Assessment Record

## 1 How to use this document

This document is the record of the Level 1 self-assessment of \<System Name\> against CSS. Its findings are at present a desk self-assessment from the documents alone (section 1.4). It has one chapter for each of the 19 control families assessed, in CSS order, and each chapter gives a finding for every determination statement. The Level 2 team re-determines every statement from this record, and \<Security Assessment Report\> brings the Level 2 results together. Every authorisation rests on the Level 2 results, not on this record alone (\<Assessment, Authorization, and Monitoring Management\>, section 3.3).

Level 1 is a self-assessment and is not independent. Each chapter holds its own assessment plan, and the authorising official approves each plan before the assessment starts.

- **Chapters.** Each chapter is named by its family code. Its sections carry the code, so PS.3 is section 3 of the Personnel Security chapter, and its tables are numbered Table PS-1, Table PS-2 and so on.
- **Citing.** Cite a chapter as \<Control Assessment Record\> (PS), a section as \<Control Assessment Record\>, section PS.3, and a table as \<Control Assessment Record\>, Table PS-1.
- **Versions.** The document has one version. A change to any chapter is a new version of the document.
- **Filling it in.** Replace each placeholder in square brackets, such as \[Result\], \[Finding\] and \[Basis\], with what the assessment found. Keep the determination statements and the plan as they stand unless the plan is changed and approved again.

### 1.1 Findings

Findings follow CSS. Each determination statement gets one of these.

**Table 1: Finding codes**

| Code | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| S | Satisfied |
| N/A | Not applicable. It is counted in the chapter's statements, but not as satisfied |
| O-D | Other than satisfied: a decision or approval is outstanding, or the control has not yet started to operate |
| O-G | Other than satisfied: a gap in the system |
| O-E | Other than satisfied: evidence from the VMs or \<Organisation\> is not available |

A finding does not by itself require a fix: each non-compliance is rated and decided in \<Risk List\>, and only those to be remediated go to \<Plan of Action and Milestones\>. An interview answer changes a finding only when the records it describes are seen. A statement found not applicable is counted in its chapter's statements but not as satisfied, and rests on the tailoring decision in \<Tailoring Register\>, which the independent assessor may test.

### 1.2 What is the same in every assessment plan

Each chapter ends with its assessment plan. These parts are the same for every family, so they are stated once here, and a chapter repeats one only where it differs.

**Table 2: Assessment plan, common to every chapter**

| Item | Detail |
| --- | --- |
| System | \<System Name\> \[version\], at the commits in \<System Security Plan\>, section 2 |
| Security categorisation | Moderate |
| Assessor | Level 1: \[Name\], working for the system owner. Level 2: an independent team led by \[Name\], which meets CA-2(1) at Moderate once each member's TPL-19 is accepted by the authorising official |
| Level 2 team and schedule | \[Name\]'s team: \[members, dates and the depth and coverage to be agreed with the independent assessor\] |
| Approval | The authorising official approves each plan before the assessment starts. Approved by: \[Name\] |

They are recorded here once, with a column for each family, so that each of the 475 statements still has its own finding. ### 1.4 Status of these findings: a desk self-assessment

The findings in this version are a desk self-assessment, made on 22 November 2026 from the document set alone, to prepare for the Level 1 and Level 2 assessments. No VM was built, no test was run, nobody was interviewed and none of \<Organisation\>'s evidence was seen. It is not the Level 1 assessment on the built VMs, which the Level 1 assessor carries out once appointed (NC-CA-02), and an authorisation never rests on it: the authorisation rests on the independent Level 2 assessment (D-CA-01). The assessment plans are not yet approved, and the assessor and dates stay to be named.

Each finding follows the control's entry in \<Risk List\>, section 5, and its line in \<System Security Plan\>, section 7, so that the three agree:

1. A control with no non-compliance entry and no tailoring entry is designed in full, and each of its statements is satisfied, with the design section as the basis.
2. A control tailored out has each statement not applicable, resting on its entry in \<Tailoring Register\> and the decision awaited in \<Decision Register\>. A parameter that applies only if selected, and is not selected, is N/S.
3. For a control with a non-compliance entry, the statements take only the causes that entry gives (D gives O-D, and E gives O-E), and each cause is found in at least one statement. A statement that defines a parameter is O-D while the parameter's approval is awaited. A statement about doing something is O-D where the entry says the step has not yet operated, and O-E where it waits on \<Organisation\>'s evidence or evidence from the VMs. An inherited control's statements are all O-E. Other statements are satisfied by the design. A control with one statement and both causes is O-D, with the evidence named in its basis.
4. No statement is O-G: no gap in the system was found, as every non-compliance in \<Risk List\> has the cause D, E or both.
5. The XX-1 statements in section 1.3 follow the same rules: the policy and procedures are written, so the statements about their content are satisfied, while their issue and first review wait on approval (O-D) and their consistency with \<Organisation\>'s policies on EV-GEN-04 (O-E).

The Level 1 assessor confirms or revises each finding on the built VMs, and the Level 2 team re-determines every statement. \<Security Assessment Report\> brings these desk results together in the same way, and is replaced by the Level 2 results.

Of the CSS 189 statements, 33 are satisfied, 17 are not applicable, 55 await a decision or approval, 0 show a gap in the system, 81 await evidence from the VMs or \<Organisation\>, and 3 are parameters not selected.

This is the desk self-assessment of section 1.4, made from the documents alone. No statement shows a gap in the system: where a control is not yet satisfied, its design is complete and it waits only on a decision, an approval, its first operation or evidence. Of the 39 entries, 2 are satisfied, 3 are not applicable and 34 are other than satisfied. The decisions most often awaited are D-AC-P04, D-AC-P52, D-AC-02 and D-AC-P05, with \<Policy\> (AC) and \<Procedures\> (AC) themselves. The evidence awaited from \<Organisation\> is EV-AC-01, EV-AC-02, EV-IA-04, EV-AC-04, EV-AC-06, EV-AC-12, EV-AC-13 and EV-AC-03, among others, as requested in \<Evidence Requests\>. The Level 2 team re-determines every statement on the built VMs (section AC.4).

### AC.3 Findings by determination statement

This section records one finding for each CSS determination statement, including those about organisation-defined parameters, in control order. Statement wording is condensed, and the identifiers are CSS's. The AC-1 statements are recorded with every family's in section 1.3.

Findings: **S** satisfied. **O-D** other than satisfied, because a decision or approval is outstanding. **O-G** other than satisfied, because of a gap in the system. **O-E** other than satisfied, because evidence from the VMs or \<Organisation\> is not available. **N/S** the parameter applies only if selected, and is not selected. **N/A** not applicable, as set out in \<Tailoring Register\>.

### AC.4 Assessment plan

This records how the assessment is planned and carried out, following CSS. It covers the Level 1 self-assessment and what remains for an independent one.

**Table AC-4: Assessment plan**

| Item | Detail |
| --- | --- |
| Scope | The 39 AC entries in the CSS Moderate baseline, with 189 determination statements |
| Procedures | CSS, using the CSS published statements and identifiers (catalog release 5.2.0) |
| Assessor | Level 1: \[Name\], working for the system owner. Level 2: an independent team led by \[Name\], which meets CA-2(1) at Moderate once each member's TPL-19 is accepted by the authorising official |
| Dates | Desk self-assessment of the documents: 22 November 2026 (section 1.4). The Level 1 assessment on the built VMs: \[Dates of the assessment\], set by the Level 1 assessor once appointed (NC-CA-02) |
| Site | Desk self-assessment: the document set only, with no build, VM, test, interview or \<Organisation\> evidence. For the assessment on the VMs: \[Where the assessment is done, and on what build and data\] |
| Examine | Policies, procedures, the SSP, \<System Description\>, the code of the UI application, Admin application, API and shared library, the access rule on each page and API function, the schema definitions and their read and edit groups, the role group settings, configuration and test results. Depth: focused. Coverage: focused, on the functions each control concerns |
| Test | Repeatable checks of access enforcement by schema and role, privilege, the group lookup in Active Directory over LDAPS, sign-in through PingFederate, the authentication cookie's absolute 8-hour lifetime, the 5-minute re-check of an open Blazor connection, Sign out, the refusal of a disabled account or removed membership within 5 minutes, the closing of a schema with no group, the refusal of item content to the Administrator role, the refusal to delete a schema that holds items, a disposal page that shows no item content, the refusal of a token request from any account other than the two applications', a browser check of the pages, and a probe of what answers without signing in. Depth: focused. Coverage: focused |
| Interview | The system owner, administrators, Data Owners, auditors and security officers, the Windows server and database administrators, developers, \<Organisation\>'s security contact, a sample of users, the business owner, the legal adviser's delegate and the Head of Cyber, using INT-AC-01 to 45. The people are named in \<Roles and Responsibilities\> |
| Previous results reused | The AC-1 statements, recorded with every family's in section 1.3. No other results are reused |
| Boundary | The whole stack on the two Windows Server 2025 VMs: the UI application, Admin application, API and shared library on the application server, and SQL Server 2022 on the database server, with Windows accounts, SQL Server logins and roles, and each application's group managed service account, as well as the Active Directory role and schema groups that give users access. \<Organisation\>'s network, remote access, devices, PingFederate and Active Directory are inherited, and Cyber Essentials Plus supports but does not prove them |
| Order of assessment | A, access enforcement (AC-3, AC-14, AC-6(10), AC-6(1)); B, privilege (AC-6, AC-6(5), AC-5, AC-6(2), AC-6(9)); C, account lifecycle (AC-2 and its enhancements, AC-6(7)); D, sign-in, sign-in lifetime and screen lock (AC-7, AC-8, AC-2(5), AC-12, AC-11, AC-11(1)), where AC-12 rests on the application's session (NFR-102) within PingFederate's sign-in, AC-2(5) on Sign out and the rule that users sign out at the end of each working day and when away longer than a short break, and AC-11 and AC-11(1) on the device screen lock; E, network and devices (AC-4, AC-17, AC-18 and AC-19 families); F, organisation (AC-20 family, AC-21, AC-22, AC-1). Access enforcement comes first because the other results depend on it |
| Needed from \<Organisation\> | Evidence for the inherited parts of AC-2(3), AC-4, AC-7, AC-11, AC-17 (including privileged access through the VPN), AC-18, AC-19, AC-20 and AC-6(9), as requested in \<Evidence Requests\> |
| Approval | The authorising official approves this plan. Approved by: \[Name\] |
| Evidence retained | The AC evidence, with its manifest; TPL-2 (the ServiceNow requests), TPL-3, TPL-14, TPL-17 and TPL-25 records; \<Access Control Management\>, section 3 |

| Test | Test-AuditAndAuth.ps1, whose audit checks cover AU and whose sign-in checks cover IA, with a hash manifest of each run. Depth: focused. Coverage: focused, including Windows and SQL Server logging on the VMs |
| Interview | The auditor, security officer, Windows server and database administrators and \<Organisation\>'s security contact, using INT-AU-01 to 07. The people are named in \<Roles and Responsibilities\> |
| Previous results reused | The AU-1 statements, recorded with every family's in section 1.3. No other results are reused |
| Boundary | The audit log in the SQL Server database, written only by the API, the application logs, and the SQL Server and Windows logs on both VMs. \<Organisation\>'s central monitoring and time service are inherited |
| Order of assessment | A, record generation and content (AU-12, AU-2, AU-3, AU-3(1)); B, protection (AU-9, AU-9(4)); C, failure, capacity and retention (AU-5, AU-4, AU-11); D, review and reporting (AU-6, AU-6(1), AU-6(3), AU-7, AU-7(1)); E, time (AU-8); F, policy (AU-1). Generation comes first because every other result depends on records existing |
| Needed from \<Organisation\> | EV-AU-01 to EV-AU-05 in \<Evidence Requests\>: central monitoring, the time source, storage and backup, the Windows audit policy, and PingFederate and Active Directory audit records |
| Evidence retained | The AU and IA evidence, with its manifest; \<Audit and Accountability Management\>, section 3 |

The independent assessor's team, schedule, and depth and coverage are added once agreed. For the Level 2 assessment, the assessor should repeat the tests on the VMs, hold the interviews, examine \<Organisation\>'s evidence and re-determine every statement in section AU.3.

| Notes | This family assesses the assessment process itself, so a self-assessment has clear limits here. |

### CM.4 Assessment plan

**Table CM-4: Assessment plan**

| Item | Detail |
| --- | --- |
| Scope | The 24 CM entries in the CSS Moderate baseline, with 178 determination statements |
| Procedures | CSS, using the CSS published statements and identifiers (catalog release 5.2.0) |
| Dates | Desk self-assessment of the documents: 22 November 2026 (section 1.4). The Level 1 assessment on the built VMs: \[Dates of the assessment\], set by the Level 1 assessor once appointed (NC-CA-02) |
| Site | Desk self-assessment: the document set only, with no build, VM, test, interview or \<Organisation\> evidence. For the assessment on the VMs: \[Where the assessment is done, and on what build and data\] |
| Examine | The build files, the .NET project files, settings files, the start-up configuration and the repositories at the release tag; \<Policy\> (CM), \<Procedures\> (CM) and \<Configuration Management\>. Depth: focused. Coverage: focused |
| Test | Test-ConfigurationManagement.ps1, with the component inventory and a hash manifest, and checks by hand, including that App Control for Business is enforced and blocks an unlisted program. Depth: focused. Coverage: focused, including the VMs, CIS settings and drift |
| Interview | The system owner, the security officer, the legal adviser, developers, the Windows server and database administrators, and \<Organisation\>'s security contact; questions in \<Interview Guide\>, Table 16 |
| Previous results reused | The CM-1 statements, recorded with every family's in section 1.3. No other results are reused |
| Boundary | The code, components and settings of \<System Name\>, and the Windows Server 2025 and SQL Server 2022 configuration of both VMs. CM-2(7) is inherited from \<Organisation\>'s travel device policy; its discovery tools, hardened build and licences are its part of shared controls |
| Order of assessment | A, baseline and inventory (CM-2, CM-8, CM-10); B, change control (CM-3, CM-4, CM-5); C, secure settings and least functionality (CM-6, CM-7 and enhancements, CM-11); D, the plan and information location (CM-9, CM-12); E, policy (CM-1). The baseline comes first because every other result is judged against it |
| Needed from \<Organisation\> | EV-CM-01 to EV-CM-05 in \<Evidence Requests\>: change restrictions on the hosts, discovery tools, licences, the hardened build and the travel device policy; and EV-AC-03 and EV-AC-05 to EV-AC-07 for the firewall rules and Group Policy settings |
| Evidence retained | The SC and CM evidence, with its manifest; \<Configuration Management\>, sections 3.4 to 3.6 |

The independent assessor's team, schedule, and depth and coverage are added once agreed. For the Level 2 assessment, the assessor should repeat the checks on the VMs, hold the interviews, examine \<Organisation\>'s evidence and re-determine every statement in section CM.3.

Of the CSS 124 statements, 28 are satisfied, 0 are not applicable, 58 await a decision or approval, 0 show a gap in the system and 38 await evidence from the VMs or \<Organisation\>.

This is the desk self-assessment of section 1.4, made from the documents alone. No statement shows a gap in the system: where a control is not yet satisfied, its design is complete and it waits only on a decision, an approval, its first operation or evidence. Of the 23 entries, 3 are satisfied, 0 are not applicable and 20 are other than satisfied. The decisions most often awaited are D-CP-P12, D-CP-P14, D-CP-02 and D-CP-01, with \<Policy\> (CP) and \<Procedures\> (CP) themselves. The evidence awaited from \<Organisation\> is EV-CP-01 and EV-CP-02, as requested in \<Evidence Requests\>. The Level 2 team re-determines every statement on the built VMs (section CP.4).

### CP.4 Assessment plan

**Table CP-4: Assessment plan**

| Item | Detail |
| --- | --- |
| Scope | The 23 CP entries in the CSS Moderate baseline, with 124 determination statements |
| Procedures | CSS, using the CSS published statements and identifiers (catalog release 5.2.0) |
| Dates | Desk self-assessment of the documents: 22 November 2026 (section 1.4). The Level 1 assessment on the built VMs: \[Dates of the assessment\], set by the Level 1 assessor once appointed (NC-CA-02) |
| Site | Desk self-assessment: the document set only, with no build, VM, test, interview or \<Organisation\> evidence. For the assessment on the VMs: \[Where the assessment is done, and on what build and data\] |
| Examine | \<Policy\> (CP), \<Procedures\> (CP) and \<Contingency Planning Management\>; the backup support and storage configuration; where items, their files and audit records are stored. Depth: focused. Coverage: focused |
| Test | Test-BackupRestore.ps1: backup, restore with its encryption certificate to the restore test server at the second site (D-CP-01), and checks of row counts, file checksums and the audit chain. Depth: focused. Coverage: focused, including backups on the VMs, the second site and a tabletop exercise |
| Interview | The system owner, the security officer, the authorising official, the legal adviser, the database and Windows server administrators, and \<Organisation\>'s security contact; questions in \<Interview Guide\>, Table 17 |
| Previous results reused | The CP-1 statements, recorded with every family's in section 1.3. No other results are reused |
| Boundary | \<System Name\>'s database, applications, secrets and the two VMs. \<Organisation\>'s second site, backup service, network and telecommunications are inherited; CP-7 is shared, as \<System Name\>'s administrators rebuild the servers at the second site |
| Order of assessment | A, recovery objectives and the plan (CP-2 and enhancements); B, backups and recovery (CP-9, CP-10 and enhancements); C, training and testing (CP-3, CP-4); D, inherited sites and telecommunications (CP-6 to CP-8); E, policy (CP-1). The objectives come first because every other result is judged against them |
| Needed from \<Organisation\> | EV-CP-01 and EV-CP-02 in \<Evidence Requests\>: the second site, the backup service, its plans and recovery times, and telecommunications |
| Evidence retained | The CP evidence, with its manifest; \<Contingency Planning Management\>, sections 3.3 and 3.8 |

The independent assessor's team, schedule, and depth and coverage are added once agreed. For the Level 2 assessment, the assessor should repeat the restore test on the VMs with encrypted backups at the second site, observe a tabletop exercise, hold the interviews, examine \<Organisation\>'s evidence and re-determine every statement in section CP.3.

Of the CSS 92 statements, 22 are satisfied, 13 are not applicable, 15 await a decision or approval, 0 show a gap in the system and 42 await evidence from the VMs or \<Organisation\>.

This is the desk self-assessment of section 1.4, made from the documents alone. No statement shows a gap in the system: where a control is not yet satisfied, its design is complete and it waits only on a decision, an approval, its first operation or evidence. Of the 24 entries, 0 are satisfied, 6 are not applicable and 18 are other than satisfied. The decisions most often awaited are D-IA-01 and D-AC-P02, with \<Policy\> (IA) and \<Procedures\> (IA) themselves. The evidence awaited from \<Organisation\> is EV-IA-04, EV-IA-01, EV-IA-02, EV-AC-01 and EV-IA-03, as requested in \<Evidence Requests\>. The Level 2 team re-determines every statement on the built VMs (section IA.4).

**Table IA-4: Assessment plan**

| Item | Detail |
| --- | --- |
| Scope | The 24 IA entries in the CSS Moderate baseline, with 92 determination statements |
| Procedures | CSS, using the CSS published statements and identifiers (catalog release 5.2.0), with CSS as the test standard for authenticators |
| Dates | Desk self-assessment of the documents: 22 November 2026 (section 1.4). The Level 1 assessment on the built VMs: \[Dates of the assessment\], set by the Level 1 assessor once appointed (NC-CA-02) |
| Site | Desk self-assessment: the document set only, with no build, VM, test, interview or \<Organisation\> evidence. For the assessment on the VMs: \[Where the assessment is done, and on what build and data\] |
| Examine | The sign-in configuration of each application and of PingFederate, the check of PingFederate's signed response, the API's group lookup in Active Directory over LDAPS, how the API learns which person each call is for, the group managed service accounts, the Windows authentication of the API to SQL Server, the key store holding the token signing key, the audit chain key and the OpenID Connect client credential, the settings files and the database, which holds no accounts or passwords, at the release tag; \<Policy\> (IA), \<Procedures\> (IA) and \<Identification and Authentication Management\>. Depth: focused. Coverage: focused |
| Test | Test-AuditAndAuth.ps1 and Test-Identification.ps1, each run with a hash manifest. Depth: focused. Coverage: focused, including Windows logon, device identification and cryptographic modules |
| Interview | Administrators, developers, the security officer, the Windows server administrator, \<Organisation\>'s security contact, IT service desk and HR, and a sample of users; questions INT-IA-01 to INT-IA-09 in \<Interview Guide\>, section 9 |
| Previous results reused | The IA-1 statements, recorded with every family's in section 1.3. No other results are reused |
| Boundary | \<System Name\>'s acceptance of PingFederate's signed response, its group lookup in Active Directory, its service accounts and the secrets in its key store. \<Organisation\>'s identity proofing, PingFederate, Active Directory and device identification are inherited |
| Order of assessment | A, authenticators (IA-5 and enhancements, IA-6, IA-7); B, sign-in and multi-factor (IA-2 and enhancements, IA-11); C, identifiers (IA-4); D, inherited proofing and devices (IA-3, IA-12); E, policy (IA-1). Authenticators come first because sign-in depends on them |
| Needed from \<Organisation\> | EV-IA-01 to EV-IA-05 in \<Evidence Requests\>: device identification, identity proofing, cryptographic modules, sign-in through PingFederate, and the group lookup in Active Directory over LDAPS |
| Evidence retained | The AU and IA evidence, with its manifest; \<Identification and Authentication Management\>, sections 3.4 to 3.6 |

The independent assessor's team, schedule, and depth and coverage are added once agreed. For the Level 2 assessment, the assessor should repeat the checks on the VMs, test multi-factor sign-in, hold the interviews, examine \<Organisation\>'s evidence and re-determine every statement in section IA.3.

Of the CSS 90 statements, 21 are satisfied, 0 are not applicable, 35 await a decision or approval, 0 show a gap in the system and 34 await evidence from the VMs or \<Organisation\>.

This is the desk self-assessment of section 1.4, made from the documents alone. No statement shows a gap in the system: where a control is not yet satisfied, its design is complete and it waits only on a decision, an approval, its first operation or evidence. Of the 13 entries, 0 are satisfied, 0 are not applicable and 13 are other than satisfied. The decisions most often awaited are D-IR-01, D-AT-P24, D-AT-P56 and D-AT-P58, with \<Policy\> (IR) and \<Procedures\> (IR) themselves. The evidence awaited from \<Organisation\> is EV-IR-01, EV-IR-02, EV-CP-01, EV-GEN-01 and EV-SR-01, as requested in \<Evidence Requests\>. The Level 2 team re-determines every statement on the built VMs (section IR.4).

### PE.4 Assessment plan

**Table PE-4: Assessment plan**

| Item | Detail |
| --- | --- |
| Scope | The 18 PE entries in the CSS Moderate baseline, with 121 determination statements, at the data centre, the second site, the offices and homes |
| Procedures | CSS, using the CSS published statements and identifiers (catalog release 5.2.0) |
| Dates | Desk self-assessment of the documents: 22 November 2026 (section 1.4). The Level 1 assessment on the built VMs: \[Dates of the assessment\], set by the Level 1 assessor once appointed (NC-CA-02) |
| Site | The main data centre and the second site named in \<Physical and Environmental Protection Management\>, section 3.2 |
| Examine | \<Policy\> (PE), \<Procedures\> (PE) and \<Physical and Environmental Protection Management\>, with the inheritance matrix; \<Security Operating Procedures\>, A33, A35 to A42 and section 4.6; \<Incident Response Management\>, section 3.9; the TPL-26 home working checks and TPL-10 physical incident records; the physical and environmental values (D-PE-P70) and D-PE-01. Depth: focused. Coverage: focused |
| Test | None for PE. Screen locking is tested under AC-11, and output marking under MP-3 |
| Interview | The security officer, a sample of home workers, \<Organisation\>'s security contact and its facilities contact (\<Interview Guide\>, INT-PE-01 to INT-PE-05) |
| Site inspection | Once at Level 2, using TPL-20, by the independent assessor or \<Organisation\>'s auditor (PRO-PE-5) |
| Previous results reused | The PE-1 statements, recorded with every family's in section 1.3. No other results are reused |
| Boundary | Physical and environmental protection of the data centre and second site is inherited from \<Organisation\>. \<System Name\> provides the policy, the printer and screen rules, home working rules, and incident handling |
| Order of assessment | A, inherited evidence against the matrix (PE-2 to PE-16); B, the site inspection; C, output devices (PE-5); D, home working (PE-17); E, policy (PE-1). The inherited evidence comes first because it closes most statements |
| Needed from \<Organisation\> | EV-PE-01 to EV-PE-05 in \<Evidence Requests\>: first its ISO/IEC 27001 certificate and Statement of Applicability for Annex A.7, or a SOC 2 report, for both sites |
| Evidence retained | \<Physical and Environmental Protection Management\>, sections 3.2 and 3.3; \<Evidence Requests\>, Table 14a; completed TPL-20 |
| Notes | Fifteen of the 18 controls are inherited from \<Organisation\>'s data centres, and \<Evidence Requests\>, Table 14a, shows the evidence that closes each one (EV-PE-01 to EV-PE-05). Screen locking is tested under AC-11, and output marking under MP-3. An unattended screen is locked by \<Organisation\>'s managed devices and the servers. \<System Name\>'s steps beside an inherited control (PE-6, PE-11, PE-16) are examined with \<Organisation\>'s evidence. PE-8(3) is assessed in the PT chapter. An inherited statement stays O-E until \<Organisation\>'s evidence is received. |

For the Level 2 assessment, the assessor should examine \<Organisation\>'s certificate, Statement of Applicability or SOC 2 report against \<Evidence Requests\>, Table 14a, inspect the main data centre and the second site with TPL-20, interview the security officer and a sample of home workers, and re-determine every statement in section PE.3.

Depth: focused. Coverage: focused |
| Test | Test-PlanConsistency.py on the document set (the PL evidence) |
| Interview | The Head of Cyber and the authorising official, on the SSP's approval; the legal adviser, on the privacy plan; the system owner, on reviews, sharing and tailoring; an administrator and a sample of users, on the rules they signed |

| Boundary | Planning is organisational. The only technical element is where the plans are kept |

| Needed from \<Organisation\> | EV-PL-01 in \<Evidence Requests\>: the enterprise architecture standards that \<System Name\> must fit, and the hypervisor |
| Evidence retained | The PL evidence (results, README and MANIFEST); the plan review records, kept in the change record (\<Documentation Map\>, section 4.1); the PL decisions in \<Decision Register\> and their sign-off sheets (TPL-24); signed TPL-21 forms and the register of signatures |
| Notes | The test is Test-PlanConsistency.py, which reads the whole document set and checks that the plans agree with each other and with the assessments. It runs on the documents only. The plans are compared with CSS, which sets out what a system plan contains, and how its approval, reviews and changes are recorded. |

For the Level 2 assessment, the assessor should run Test-PlanConsistency.py on the issued set, sample signed TPL-21 forms against the register and the access requests, check the restricted store's access list, interview the Head of Cyber, the authorising official and a sample of users, and re-determine every statement in section PL.3.

Of the CSS 68 statements, 19 are satisfied, 0 are not applicable, 28 await a decision or approval, 0 show a gap in the system and 21 await evidence from the VMs or \<Organisation\>.

This is the desk self-assessment of section 1.4, made from the documents alone. No statement shows a gap in the system: where a control is not yet satisfied, its design is complete and it waits only on a decision, an approval, its first operation or evidence. Of the 9 entries, 0 are satisfied, 0 are not applicable and 9 are other than satisfied. The decisions most often awaited are D-PS-P72, D-AC-P06, D-PS-02 and D-PS-01, with \<Policy\> (PS) and \<Procedures\> (PS) themselves. The evidence awaited from \<Organisation\> is EV-PS-02, EV-AC-01, EV-PS-01 and EV-PS-03, as requested in \<Evidence Requests\>. The Level 2 team re-determines every statement on the built VMs (section PS.4).

For the Level 2 assessment, the assessor should:

- sample people with access against the personnel security register, TPL-2 and the signed agreements;
- follow one leaver, one higher-risk leaver or urgent disable, and one mover from notice to TPL-14, and compare the times with the audit records and the required times: memberships removed within 5 working days, an urgent disable within 1 hour, and a supplier's mover or leaver notice within 1 working day;
- check that everyone in a higher risk role was screened or rescreened within the last 5 years;
- examine EV-PS-01 to EV-PS-03;
- hold the interviews, and re-determine every statement in section PS.3.

### PT.4 Assessment plan

**Table PT-4: Assessment plan**

| Item | Detail |
| --- | --- |
| Security categorisation | Moderate, with the privacy baseline applied |
| Scope | 5 PT controls and 15 privacy baseline controls outside Moderate, with 103 determination statements, covering every kind of personal data in \<PII Processing Management\>, section 3.3 |
| Procedures | CSS, using the CSS published statements and identifiers (catalog release 5.2.0); UK GDPR, the DPA 2018 and the Data (Use and Access) Act 2025 for what the organisation must comply with |
| Dates | Desk self-assessment of the documents: 22 November 2026 (section 1.4). The Level 1 assessment on the built VMs: \[Dates of the assessment\], set by the Level 1 assessor once appointed (NC-CA-02) |
| Examine | \<Policy\> (PT), \<Procedures\> (PT) and \<PII Processing Management\>, \<Data Protection Impact Assessment\>, \<Privacy Plan\>, \<Incident Response Management\>, section 3.8, \<Security Operating Procedures\>, A23 to A29 and C33; \<System Description\>, section 4, for the request time, the laws that apply and the privacy values; \<Decision Register\>, D-PT-01 to D-PT-07 and D-PT-P22, D-PT-P31 and D-PT-P73; TPL-3, TPL-15, TPL-22 and TPL-25 records; the audit record format, the item management functions, the correct and delete functions, and the search across schemas for one person's data (\<System Description\>, FR-073). Depth: focused. Coverage: focused |
| Test | Test-PersonalData.ps1, on a fresh throwaway stack (the PT evidence) |
| Interview | The legal adviser, the business owner, the data protection officer, a Data Owner, an editor, the system owner, the security officer and the legal adviser's delegate who would handle a request (INT-PT-01 to INT-PT-12) |
| Previous results reused | The PT-1 statements, recorded with every family's in section 1.3. No other results are reused |
| Boundary | \<System Name\>'s handling of personal data. \<Organisation\>'s notice, record of processing and privacy programme are inherited (EV-PT-01, EV-PM-02, EV-PM-04) |
| Order of assessment | A, the impact assessment and lawful bases (RA-8, PT-2, PT-7); B, people's rights (AC-3(14), SI-18(4)); C, retention (SI-12(3)); D, transparency (PT-5); E, logs (AU-3(3)); F, policy (PT-1). The impact assessment comes first because most other findings depend on it |
| Needed from \<Organisation\> | EV-PT-01, EV-PT-02, EV-PM-02 and EV-PM-04 in \<Evidence Requests\> |
| Evidence retained | The PT evidence (results, README and MANIFEST); completed TPL-15 and TPL-22 records; \<Data Protection Impact Assessment\> |
| Notes | The test is Test-PersonalData.ps1, run on the release and a fresh throwaway stack. Legal positions come from \<Data Protection Impact Assessment\> and \<PII Processing Management\>, section 3.3, which the legal adviser completes. |

For the Level 2 assessment, the assessor should examine the completed impact assessment and the lawful bases, run Test-PersonalData.ps1 on the VMs, walk through a sample subject access request and complaint with the legal adviser, interview the business owner on retention, and re-determine every statement in section PT.3.

### RA.4 Assessment plan

**Table RA-4: Assessment plan**

| Item | Detail |
| --- | --- |
| Scope | The 10 RA entries in the Moderate baseline, with 73 determination statements |
| Procedures | CSS, using the CSS published statements and identifiers (catalog release 5.2.0); CSS for the risk method |
| Dates | Desk self-assessment of the documents: 22 November 2026 (section 1.4). The Level 1 assessment on the built VMs: \[Dates of the assessment\], set by the Level 1 assessor once appointed (NC-CA-02) |
| Examine | \<Policy\> (RA), \<Procedures\> (RA) and \<Risk Assessment Management\>; \<Risk List\>, with its threat model; \<System Security Plan\>, section 3; \<System Description\>, section 4, for the categorisation, flaw fixing, vulnerability scanning and risk assessment values; \<Supply Chain Risk Management\>; \<Data Protection Impact Assessment\>, section 6; \<Decision Register\>, D-RA-01 to D-RA-04; \<Plan of Action and Milestones\>; TPL-7 and TPL-23 records. Depth: focused. Coverage: focused |
| Test | Test-Vulnerability.ps1, on the release tag and the servers (the RA evidence) |
| Interview | The system owner, the authorising official, the security officer, the Windows server administrator, the developers and \<Organisation\>'s security contact |
| Previous results reused | The RA-1 statements, recorded with every family's in section 1.3. No other results are reused |
| Boundary | \<System Name\>'s risk assessment and vulnerability management. \<Organisation\>'s risk strategy, scanning services and virtual platform are inherited (EV-RA-01, EV-PM-01) |
| Order of assessment | A, vulnerabilities (RA-5, RA-5(2), RA-5(5)); B, risk method and register (RA-3, RA-7); C, supply chain and critical components (RA-3(1), RA-9); D, categorisation (RA-2); E, policy (RA-1) |
| Needed from \<Organisation\> | EV-RA-01 and EV-PM-01 in \<Evidence Requests\> |
| Evidence retained | The RA evidence (results, README and MANIFEST); completed TPL-7 and TPL-23 records |
| Notes | The test is Test-Vulnerability.ps1, run on the release and the two Windows Server 2025 VMs. It checks the release's packages, the software installed on the servers, and whether the risk list, decisions, acceptances, critical components and plan of action agree. |

For the Level 2 assessment, the assessor should run Test-Vulnerability.ps1 and a credentialed scan on the VMs, sample TPL-7 records against the timetable, interview the security officer on an exploited flaw, and re-determine every statement in section RA.3.

## SA System and Services Acquisition

### SA.1 Purpose

This records the Level 1 self-assessment of the 17 SA entries in the CSS Moderate baseline, using catalog release 5.2.0. SA-4(10) is proposed as not applicable in \<Tailoring Register\>, TR-SA-01, and is assessed against its condition. An independent team led by \[Name\] will use it for the Level 2 assessment. It makes no fixes.

Of the CSS 160 statements, 52 are satisfied, 1 are not applicable, 47 await a decision or approval, 0 show a gap in the system and 60 await evidence from the VMs or \<Organisation\>.

This is the desk self-assessment of section 1.4, made from the documents alone. No statement shows a gap in the system: where a control is not yet satisfied, its design is complete and it waits only on a decision, an approval, its first operation or evidence. Of the 17 entries, 0 are satisfied, 1 are not applicable and 16 are other than satisfied. The decisions most often awaited are D-SA-P75, D-PL-01, D-SA-P28 and D-RA-04, with \<Policy\> (SA) and \<Procedures\> (SA) themselves. The evidence awaited from \<Organisation\> is EV-SA-01, EV-SA-03, EV-SA-04, EV-CA-02, EV-SA-02 and EV-MA-01, as requested in \<Evidence Requests\>, and 4 statements also await evidence from the built VMs. The Level 2 team re-determines every statement on the built VMs (section SA.4).

## SC System and Communications Protection

Of the CSS 107 statements, 28 are satisfied, 3 are not applicable, 22 await a decision or approval, 0 show a gap in the system and 54 await evidence from the VMs or \<Organisation\>.

This is the desk self-assessment of section 1.4, made from the documents alone. No statement shows a gap in the system: where a control is not yet satisfied, its design is complete and it waits only on a decision, an approval, its first operation or evidence. Of the 25 entries, 0 are satisfied, 1 are not applicable and 24 are other than satisfied. The decisions most often awaited are D-SC-02, D-SC-P26, D-AC-P02 and D-IA-01, with \<Policy\> (SC) and \<Procedures\> (SC) themselves. The evidence awaited from \<Organisation\> is EV-SC-02, EV-SC-05, EV-SC-03, EV-AU-01, EV-IA-04, EV-SC-01, EV-SC-04 and EV-GEN-03, as requested in \<Evidence Requests\>, and 4 statements also await evidence from the built VMs. The Level 2 team re-determines every statement on the built VMs (section SC.4).

| Interview | The Windows server administrator, database administrator, developers and \<Organisation\>'s security contact |
| Previous results reused | The SC-1 statements, recorded with every family's in section 1.3. No other results are reused |
| Boundary | \<System Name\>'s UI application, Admin application, API, SQL Server database, keys and the two VMs, and flows F1 to F8, F10 and F11 in \<System Description\>, section 6.5, with the infrastructure and scanning rules on the VMs. \<Organisation\>'s network, VPN, proxy, DNS, certificate authority and hypervisor are provider evidence (EV-SC-01 to EV-SC-05, EV-GEN-03) |
| Order of assessment | A, transport, network disconnect and the authenticity of connections (SC-8, SC-8(1), SC-10, SC-23); B, keys, cryptography and certificates (SC-12, SC-13, SC-17); C, page code (SC-18); D, boundary and separation (SC-2, SC-7 and its enhancements); E, data at rest (SC-28, SC-28(1)); F, the servers' own protections (SC-4, SC-5, SC-39); G, \<Organisation\>'s name resolution (SC-20 to SC-22); H, policy (SC-1); SC-15 by its tailoring (TR-SC-01) |
| Needed from providers | EV-SC-01 to EV-SC-05, EV-AC-03, EV-GEN-03 and EV-IA-04 in \<Evidence Requests\> |
| Evidence retained | The SC evidence (results, README and MANIFEST); the SC and CM evidence; completed TPL-6, TPL-8, TPL-11 and TPL-23 records |
| Notes | The test is Test-Protection.ps1, run against a throwaway test stack built from the release. It sends requests to the web addresses, runs read-only queries in the database, and reads the code and settings, printing counts and lengths only. |

For the Level 2 assessment, the assessor should run Test-Protection.ps1 against the VMs, run an outside TLS scan of the web address, check the host firewall rules against \<System and Communications Protection Management\>, section 3.2, check each VM's trust stores against \<Organisation\>'s approved trust anchors, confirm BitLocker and the key ring protection, hold the interviews, and re-determine every statement in section SC.3.

## SI System and Information Integrity

### SI.1 Purpose

This records the Level 1 self-assessment of the 18 SI entries in the CSS Moderate baseline, using catalog release 5.2.0. SI-8 and SI-8(2) are covered by tailoring proposal TR-SI-01 in \<Tailoring Register\>. An independent team led by \[Name\] will use it for the Level 2 assessment. It makes no fixes.

Of the CSS 139 statements, 34 are satisfied, 7 are not applicable, 45 await a decision or approval, 0 show a gap in the system and 53 await evidence from the VMs or \<Organisation\>.

This is the desk self-assessment of section 1.4, made from the documents alone. No statement shows a gap in the system: where a control is not yet satisfied, its design is complete and it waits only on a decision, an approval, its first operation or evidence. Of the 18 entries, 0 are satisfied, 2 are not applicable and 16 are other than satisfied. The decisions most often awaited are D-CM-P29, D-SI-P27, D-SI-02 and D-PT-05, with \<Policy\> (SI) and \<Procedures\> (SI) themselves. The evidence awaited from \<Organisation\> is EV-SI-01, EV-SI-02 and EV-SI-03, as requested in \<Evidence Requests\>, and 15 statements also await evidence from the built VMs. The Level 2 team re-determines every statement on the built VMs (section SI.4).

| Previous results reused | The SI-1 statements, recorded with every family's in section 1.3. No other results are reused |
| Boundary | \<System Name\>'s UI application, Admin application, API, SQL Server database and records. \<Organisation\>'s network monitoring, firmware and logging are provider evidence (EV-SI-01 to EV-SI-03) |
| Order of assessment | A, flaws (SI-2); B, malicious code and uploads (SI-3, SI-10); C, monitoring and alerts (SI-4); D, integrity (SI-7); E, errors (SI-11); F, advisories, retention and memory (SI-5, SI-12, SI-16); G, policy (SI-1) |
| Needed from providers | EV-SI-01, EV-SI-02 and EV-SI-03 in \<Evidence Requests\> |
| Evidence retained | The SI evidence (results, baseline, README and MANIFEST); the RA evidence; completed TPL-7, TPL-8 and TPL-10 records |
| Notes | The test is Test-Integrity.ps1, run against a throwaway test stack built from the release. Upload checks use the API's check-only function for a batch, which stores nothing (\<System Description\>, FR-042). Package checks come from Test-Vulnerability.ps1 (the RA evidence). |

For the Level 2 assessment, the assessor should run Test-Integrity.ps1 and Test-Vulnerability.ps1 against the VMs, upload the EICAR test file, alone and in a ZIP file, to check malware scanning, check Defender, its exclusions and the alert rules, compare the installed release files with the release record, hold the interviews, and re-determine every statement in section SI.3.

## SR Supply Chain Risk Management

### SR.1 Purpose

This records the Level 1 self-assessment of the 12 SR entries in the CSS Moderate baseline, using catalog release 5.2.0. An independent team led by \[Name\] will use it for the Level 2 assessment. It makes no fixes.

Of the CSS 81 statements, 38 are satisfied, 0 are not applicable, 28 await a decision or approval, 0 show a gap in the system and 15 await evidence from the VMs or \<Organisation\>.

This is the desk self-assessment of section 1.4, made from the documents alone. No statement shows a gap in the system: where a control is not yet satisfied, its design is complete and it waits only on a decision, an approval, its first operation or evidence. Of the 12 entries, 0 are satisfied, 0 are not applicable and 12 are other than satisfied. The decisions most often awaited are D-SR-03, D-SR-01, D-SR-P76 and D-SR-02, with \<Policy\> (SR) and \<Procedures\> (SR) themselves. The evidence awaited from \<Organisation\> is EV-SR-01, EV-SR-02, EV-MA-01, EV-SA-01 and EV-SA-04, as requested in \<Evidence Requests\>. The Level 2 team re-determines every statement on the built VMs (section SR.4).

**Table SR-4: Assessment plan**

| Item | Detail |
| --- | --- |

| Procedures | CSS, using the CSS published statements and identifiers (catalog release 5.2.0) |
| Dates | Desk self-assessment of the documents: 22 November 2026 (section 1.4). The Level 1 assessment on the built VMs: \[Dates of the assessment\], set by the Level 1 assessor once appointed (NC-CA-02) |
| Examine | \<Policy\> (SR), \<Procedures\> (SR) and \<Supply Chain Risk Management\>; \<Risk Assessment Management\>, sections 3.9 and 3.4; \<Configuration Management\>, Table 4; the package source settings files, the build files, each application's restored package list, the browser library manifest, the release record and the in-house package feed's index at the release tag; completed TPL-16 supplier reviews. Depth: focused. Coverage: focused |
| Test | Test-SupplyChain.ps1, with no running system (the SR evidence); Test-Vulnerability.ps1 (the RA evidence) |
| Interview | The system owner, the developers, the security officer and \<Organisation\>'s IT service desk; questions INT-SR-01 to 07 in \<Interview Guide\> |
| Previous results reused | The SR-1 statements, recorded with every family's in section 1.3. No other results are reused |
| Boundary | \<System Name\>'s code, build and release. \<Organisation\>'s suppliers, hardware, repair and disposal are provider evidence (EV-SR-01, EV-SR-02) |
| Order of assessment | A, sources and acquisition (SR-3, SR-5); B, inspection (SR-10); C, suppliers and notices (SR-6, SR-8); D, authenticity (SR-11); E, plan and team (SR-2); F, disposal (SR-12); G, policy (SR-1) |
| Needed from providers | EV-SR-01 and EV-SR-02 in \<Evidence Requests\>; EV-SA-01, the contract terms; EV-SA-04, the corporate SDLC's feed and pipeline; EV-MA-01, hardware sent for repair; EV-PM-03, \<Organisation\>'s strategy and critical suppliers |
| Evidence retained | The SR evidence (results, README and MANIFEST); the RA evidence; the release record, bill of materials and browser library manifest of the release assessed; completed TPL-6, TPL-7, TPL-10, TPL-13 and TPL-16 records |
| Notes | The test is Test-SupplyChain.ps1, run on the release and the packages built from it, with no running system. \<Organisation\>'s supplier, hardware and disposal processes are shown by its evidence. |

For the Level 2 assessment, the assessor should run Test-SupplyChain.ps1 on the release to be deployed, check the bill of materials and release SHA-256 values, examine \<Organisation\>'s supplier, hardware and disposal evidence, hold the interviews, and re-determine every statement in section SR.3.
